### Primary Outputs
• Approved Risk Register
• Active Risk Inventory
• Risk Status
### Supporting Outputs
• Risk visibility.
• Risk monitoring.
• Decision support.
• Input to governance reviews.
---
## Dependencies
This document depends upon:
• Approved Initial Risk Assessment.
• Approved Production Plan.
• Approved Production Schedule.
• Approved Resource Plan.
• Approved Budget Plan.
---
## Relationships
### Upstream Documents
• Document 17 — Initial Risk Assessment
• Document 28 — Production Plan
• Document 29 — Production Schedule

• Document 30 — Resource Plan
• Document 31 — Budget Plan
### Downstream Documents
• Document 34 — Production Progress Report
• Document 35 — Issue Register
• Change Management Documents
• Production Closure Documents
### Supporting Documents
• Decision Log
• Lessons Learned Register
• Review Records
• Audit Records
---
## Required Sections
The Risk Register shall include, where applicable:
• Risk Identifier
• Risk Description
• Risk Category
• Risk Owner
• Probability
• Impact
• Risk Rating
• Response Strategy
• Current Status
• Review Date
---
## Optional Sections
Where appropriate, the Risk Register may additionally include:
• Risk Trigger
• Mitigation Notes

• Residual Risk
• Escalation Status
• Historical Changes
Optional sections shall supplement the approved register without replacing required risk information.
---
## Standard Document Structure
The Risk Register shall follow the standardized metadata architecture established by the Production Documentation
System.
The operational specification defined by this document supplements that architecture.
---
## Lifecycle
The Risk Register progresses through:
• Creation
• Review
• Approval
• Operational Use
• Continuous Update
• Controlled Revision
• Archiving
The register shall remain active throughout the production lifecycle.
---
## Revision Rules
This document may be revised only to:
• Register new approved risks.
• Update existing risks.
• Close resolved risks.
• Correct approved information.
• Reflect approved governance decisions.
Every revision shall remain documented, approved where required, and traceable.
---

## Usage Rules
Every official Explorer Studio production shall possess one approved Risk Register.
The register shall remain current throughout production.
Closed risks shall remain recorded for historical traceability.
---
## Governance Notes
This document owns the operational standard for production risk registration.
No other production document may redefine or replace this responsibility.
The Risk Register records and monitors production risks.
It does not perform risk assessments or manage production issues.
---
## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:
• Record risks immediately after identification.
• Maintain one authoritative risk register.
• Review risks regularly.
• Assign clear ownership.
• Monitor risk status continuously.
• Preserve complete historical traceability.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Records all approved production risks.
• Identifies risk ownership.
• Maintains current risk status.
• Supports production governance.
• Supports production decision-making.
• Establishes the official production risk baseline.
---

# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Risk Register
The Risk Register serves as the authoritative operational repository for production risks.
It records the complete lifecycle of each approved production risk from identification through closure.
The Risk Register defines **what production risks currently exist**.
It does not determine **how risks are initially assessed or how operational issues are managed**.
---
## 2. Core Principles
The Risk Register operates according to the following principles.
### Continuous Visibility
Production risks shall remain visible throughout the production lifecycle.
Risk information shall remain current.
---
### Accountability
Every significant production risk shall possess an identified owner responsible for monitoring the risk.
Ownership supports accountability.
It does not transfer organizational authority.
---
### Consistency
Risks shall be classified and maintained using standardized organizational methods.
Equivalent risks shall receive equivalent treatment.
---
### Traceability
Every risk update shall remain traceable throughout its complete lifecycle.
Historical information shall be preserved.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Risk Register establishes the following operational components.
### Risk Identification
Every approved production risk shall receive a unique identifier.

The identifier supports governance, monitoring, and reporting.
---
### Risk Classification
Risks shall be categorized using the approved organizational classification framework.
Classification supports reporting and analysis.
---
### Risk Ownership
Each risk shall identify the operational owner responsible for monitoring its status.
Ownership supports governance.
It does not replace organizational responsibility.
---
### Risk Evaluation
The register records the approved probability, impact, and overall risk rating.
Evaluation values originate from approved risk assessment activities.
The register maintains—not creates—the approved evaluation.
---
### Response Strategy
The register records the approved response strategy associated with each production risk.
Typical response strategies may include:
• Avoid
• Reduce
• Transfer
• Accept
The register records the strategy.
Implementation belongs to operational management.
---
### Risk Status
Each risk shall maintain a current operational status, including:
• Open

• Monitoring
• Mitigating
• Escalated
• Closed
Additional approved organizational statuses may be used where applicable.
---
### Review History
Risk reviews shall remain documented throughout the production lifecycle.
Historical review information supports governance and continuous improvement.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
The Risk Register shall remain active throughout:
• Production Planning.
• Production Execution.
• Production Monitoring.
• Production Closure.
Risk information shall be reviewed regularly according to approved governance processes.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Risk Register is responsible for:
• Recording production risks.
• Maintaining risk information.
• Recording ownership.
• Recording approved evaluations.
• Recording approved response strategies.
• Monitoring risk status.
It is not responsible for:
• Performing Initial Risk Assessments.
• Managing production issues.
• Authorizing mitigation actions.

• Managing production execution.
• Managing organizational governance.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## 6. Operational Constraints
The Risk Register shall not:
• Replace the Initial Risk Assessment.
• Replace Issue Management.
• Replace Change Management.
• Replace Production Governance.
• Replace Production Progress Reporting.
Its responsibility is limited to maintaining the official operational record of production risks.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Risk Register establishes the official operational repository for production risks within an Explorer Studio
production.
It records approved risks, maintains ownership, tracks status, preserves review history, and supports governance
through continuous visibility and traceability.
The approved Risk Register becomes the authoritative specification for production risk monitoring while preserving the
Single Source of Truth for all production risk information throughout the production lifecycle.

# EXPLORER STUDIO PRODUCTION DOCUMENTATION SYSTEM
# PART III — PRE-PRODUCTION DOCUMENTS
# Document 34 — Dependency Register
---
## Purpose
The Dependency Register defines the official operational record of production dependencies for an Explorer Studio
production.
Its purpose is to identify, classify, document, monitor, and maintain every internal and external dependency that may affect
production planning, execution, scheduling, coordination, delivery, or successful completion.
The Dependency Register serves as the authoritative inventory of production dependencies.
It records production dependencies.
It does not manage production schedules, assign resources, approve changes, or manage production execution.
This document establishes the official production dependency management baseline for every Explorer Studio production.
---
## Scope
This document applies to every official Explorer Studio production.
It governs production dependency identification and tracking only.
It does not replace the Production Plan, Production Schedule, Resource Plan, Risk Register, Production Task Specification, or
production execution management.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## When Used
This document shall be used:
• During production planning.
• Before production execution.
• Whenever new dependencies are identified.
• During production monitoring.
• During production reviews.
• Until production closure.
---

## Owner
Explorer Studio
Production Manager
---
## Contributors

• Production Manager
• Documentation Manager
• Department Leads
• Technical Leads
• Creative Leads
• Task Owners
• Future authorized production roles
---
## Approval Authority
Official approval shall be granted by the constitutional authority responsible for production planning governance.
---
## Inputs
### Constitutional Inputs
• Explorer Studio Brand Bible
• Explorer Studio Documentation Standards
• Explorer Studio Production Bible
### Operational Inputs

• Asset Specifications
---

• Dependency Status
### Supporting Outputs

• Input to risk management.
• Production coordination.
• Dependency traceability.
---

• Approved production task definitions.
---

---

## Lifecycle

• Review
• Approval
• Operational Monitoring
• Status Updates
• Resolution
• Archiving

---
## Revision Rules

---
## Usage Rules
Every official Explorer Studio production shall maintain one approved Dependency Register.
Dependencies shall be recorded as soon as they are identified.
Dependency status shall remain current throughout production.
---
## Governance Notes
This document owns the operational standard for production dependency management.
No other production document may redefine or replace this responsibility.
The Dependency Register records and monitors production dependencies.
It does not manage production schedules or production execution.
---

## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:
• Identify dependencies early.
• Assign clear dependency ownership.
• Monitor dependencies regularly.
• Link dependencies to affected production activities.
• Review dependency impacts throughout production.
• Preserve complete dependency traceability.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Records all significant production dependencies.
• Identifies dependency ownership.
• Maintains current dependency status.
• Documents dependency impacts.
• Supports production planning.
• Establishes the official dependency baseline.
---
# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Dependency Register
The Dependency Register establishes the official repository of production dependencies.
It provides a structured method for identifying and monitoring the relationships between production activities, assets,
external inputs, approvals, and organizational requirements that influence production success.
The Dependency Register defines **what production activities depend upon**.
It does not define **how production work is scheduled or executed**.
---
## 2. Core Principles
The Dependency Register operates according to the following principles.
### Visibility
Every significant production dependency shall remain visible throughout the production lifecycle.
Dependency status shall remain continuously monitored.

---
### Accountability
Every dependency shall identify one operational owner responsible for monitoring and coordinating its resolution.
Ownership supports accountability without transferring execution authority.
---
### Traceability
Every dependency shall remain traceable to related production tasks, assets, approvals, and supporting documentation.
---
### Consistency
Dependencies shall be identified, classified, and managed using one approved organizational standard.
Equivalent dependencies shall receive equivalent documentation.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Dependency Register establishes the following operational components.
### Dependency Identification
Each dependency shall receive a unique Dependency Identifier.
The identifier supports governance, reporting, scheduling, and operational traceability.
---
### Dependency Classification
Each dependency shall belong to one approved dependency category.
Typical categories may include:
• Internal Dependency
• External Dependency
• Technical Dependency
• Creative Dependency
• Resource Dependency
• Approval Dependency
Organizations may define additional approved classifications where appropriate.
---
### Dependency Ownership
Every dependency shall identify the individual or organizational role responsible for monitoring the dependency.

---
### Dependency Status
Each dependency shall maintain an approved operational status, including where applicable:
• Identified
• Pending
• Active
• Satisfied
• Delayed
• Closed
Additional approved organizational statuses may be adopted where appropriate.
---
### Impact Assessment
Each dependency shall include a summary of its potential impact upon production if not satisfied.
Impact supports planning and coordination.
---
### Mitigation Notes
Where appropriate, mitigation information may be recorded to support dependency management.
Detailed risk ownership remains with the Risk Register.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
Every significant production dependency shall:
• Be recorded after identification.
• Be assigned an operational owner.
• Be monitored until resolution.
• Be reviewed during production planning and execution.
Dependency information shall remain aligned with production planning documentation.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Dependency Register is responsible for:
• Recording production dependencies.

• Recording dependency ownership.
• Recording dependency status.
• Recording dependency impacts.
• Supporting production planning.
• Supporting production coordination.
It is not responsible for:
• Managing production schedules.
• Managing production execution.
• Managing production risks.
• Managing production resources.
• Approving production changes.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## 6. Operational Constraints
The Dependency Register shall not:
• Replace the Production Plan.
• Replace the Production Schedule.
• Replace the Risk Register.
• Replace Production Task Specifications.
• Replace production execution management.
Its responsibility is limited to maintaining the official inventory of production dependencies.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Dependency Register establishes the official operational repository of production dependencies within an Explorer Studio
production.
It records internal and external dependencies, maintains ownership, classification, operational status, impact assessments,
and supporting references while providing complete visibility and traceability throughout the production lifecycle.
The approved Dependency Register becomes the authoritative specification for dependency management while preserving
the Single Source of Truth for all production dependencies across the production lifecycle.

# EXPLORER STUDIO PRODUCTION DOCUMENTATION SYSTEM
# PART III — PRE-PRODUCTION DOCUMENTS
# Document 35 — Pre-Production Review Report
---
## Purpose
The Pre-Production Review Report defines the official operational assessment of pre-production readiness for an Explorer
Studio production.
Its purpose is to verify that all required pre-production documentation, planning activities, creative development, technical
preparation, resource planning, dependencies, risks, and governance requirements have been satisfactorily completed before
authorizing transition into active production.
The Pre-Production Review Report serves as the authoritative readiness assessment for production commencement.
It evaluates production readiness.
It does not authorize production, redefine production plans, or replace production governance.
This document establishes the official pre-production review standard for every Explorer Studio production.
---
## Scope
This document applies to every official Explorer Studio production.
It governs pre-production readiness assessment only.
It does not replace the Production Approval Record, Production Plan, Production Schedule, Risk Register, Dependency
Register, or production execution.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## When Used
This document shall be used:
• After completion of pre-production.
• Before active production begins.
• During executive readiness reviews.
• Before production authorization.
• During governance reviews.
• Whenever formal production readiness assessment is required.

---
## Owner
Explorer Studio
Production Manager
---
## Contributors
Where applicable, contributors may include:
• Executive Producer
• Production Manager
• Documentation Manager
• Creative Director
• Technical Director
• Department Leads
• Quality Representatives
• Future authorized production roles
---
## Approval Authority
Official approval shall be granted by the constitutional authority responsible for production governance.

• Approved production planning.
• Approved technical preparation.
• Completed readiness reviews.

## Revision Rules
This document may be revised only to:

• Correct factual review findings.
Historical review conclusions shall not be altered without approved governance.
Every revision shall remain documented and traceable.
---
## Usage Rules
Every official Explorer Studio production shall complete one Pre-Production Review Report before entering active production.
The review shall be based upon verified production documentation and operational evidence.
Recommendations shall remain objective and evidence-based.

---
## Governance Notes
This document owns the operational standard for pre-production readiness assessment.
No other production document may redefine or replace this responsibility.
The Pre-Production Review Report evaluates production readiness.
It does not authorize production commencement.
---
## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:
• Review every required pre-production document.
• Verify production readiness objectively.
• Record measurable findings.
• Identify unresolved issues clearly.
• Preserve complete review evidence.
• Maintain complete governance traceability.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Reviews all required pre-production documentation.
• Evaluates creative readiness.
• Evaluates technical readiness.
• Evaluates operational readiness.
• Records review findings.
• Establishes the official production readiness assessment.
---
# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Pre-Production Review Report
The Pre-Production Review Report establishes the official readiness assessment performed before production execution
begins.
It evaluates whether the production has completed the required planning, creative development, technical preparation,
documentation, and governance activities necessary to proceed into production.

The Pre-Production Review Report defines **whether the production is operationally ready to begin**.
It does not define **whether production is formally authorized to begin**.
---
## 2. Core Principles
The Pre-Production Review Report operates according to the following principles.
### Objectivity
Readiness assessments shall be based upon verified production evidence.
Review conclusions shall remain factual and measurable.
---
### Completeness
Every required pre-production discipline shall be reviewed before readiness is determined.
Incomplete preparation shall remain explicitly identified.
---
### Traceability
Every review finding shall remain traceable to approved production documentation.
Supporting evidence shall remain available for governance review.
---
### Governance Support
The report shall support production governance by providing an objective readiness assessment.
Governance decisions remain outside the responsibility of this document.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Pre-Production Review Report establishes the following operational components.
### Documentation Readiness
Evaluates whether all required production documentation has been completed and approved.
---
### Creative Readiness
Evaluates the readiness of approved creative development, including concept, world, story, character, visual, and audio
development.
---
### Technical Readiness

Evaluates the readiness of technical architecture, production pipeline, implementation standards, and supporting technical
preparation.
---
### Operational Readiness
Evaluates production planning, scheduling, resources, budget preparation, dependency management, and production
organization.
---
### Outstanding Items
Identifies incomplete activities requiring resolution before production begins.
---
### Review Findings
Records objective observations resulting from the readiness assessment.
---
### Review Recommendation
Provides one of the following recommendations where applicable:
• Ready for Production
• Ready with Conditions
• Further Preparation Required
Recommendation supports governance but does not authorize production.
---
### Overall Readiness Status
Provides the final operational assessment of production readiness.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
The Pre-Production Review Report shall be completed before:
• Production Task execution.
• Asset production.
• Active production operations.
Every review shall be evidence-based and supported by approved production documentation.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Pre-Production Review Report is responsible for:

• Assessing production readiness.
• Reviewing required documentation.
• Recording review findings.
• Recording readiness recommendations.
• Supporting production governance.
• Establishing the official readiness assessment.
It is not responsible for:
• Authorizing production.
• Revising production documentation.
• Managing production execution.
• Managing production risks.
• Managing production schedules.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## 6. Operational Constraints
The Pre-Production Review Report shall not:
• Replace the Production Approval Record.
• Replace the Production Plan.
• Replace the Risk Register.
• Replace the Dependency Register.
• Replace executive governance.
Its responsibility is limited to assessing pre-production readiness.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Pre-Production Review Report establishes the official readiness assessment for an Explorer Studio production.
It evaluates documentation, creative development, technical preparation, operational planning, dependencies, risks, and
governance readiness before production begins, while recording objective findings, outstanding items, and readiness
recommendations.
The approved Pre-Production Review Report becomes the authoritative specification for production readiness assessment
while preserving the Single Source of Truth for determining operational readiness before transition into active production.

# EXPLORER STUDIO PRODUCTION DOCUMENTATION SYSTEM
# PART IV — PRODUCTION DOCUMENTS
# Document 36 — Production Task Specification
---
## Purpose
The Production Task Specification defines the official operational specification for an individual production task within an
Explorer Studio production.
Its purpose is to establish the objective, scope, inputs, outputs, requirements, dependencies, acceptance criteria, and
execution constraints for a specific production task, ensuring that every production activity is clearly defined before
execution begins.
The Production Task Specification serves as the authoritative definition of an individual production task.
It defines production work.
It does not assign personnel, schedule work, approve deliverables, or manage execution.
This document establishes the official production task specification standard for every Explorer Studio production.
---
## Scope
This document applies to every official Explorer Studio production.
It governs production task specification only.
It does not replace the Production Plan, Production Schedule, Resource Plan, Asset Specification, Production Checklist, or
production execution management.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## When Used
This document shall be used:
• Before a production task begins.
• Before production resource allocation.
• Before production verification.
• During production execution.
• Whenever a new production task is introduced.

• Whenever a task specification requires approved revision.

## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:
• Define tasks before execution.
• Keep task responsibilities unambiguous.
• Maintain measurable acceptance criteria.

• Avoid combining multiple operational responsibilities within one specification.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Defines one production task.
• Establishes task requirements.
• Defines task inputs and outputs.
• Establishes measurable acceptance criteria.
• Supports production execution.
• Establishes the official task definition.
---
# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Production Task Specification
The Production Task Specification establishes the official definition of an individual production task.
It provides the operational information necessary to perform one approved production activity consistently and in
alignment with approved production baselines.
The Production Task Specification defines **what work shall be performed**.
It does not define **who performs the work or when it is performed**.
---
## 2. Core Principles

The Production Task Specification operates according to the following principles.
### Single Responsibility
Each Production Task Specification shall define one operational task.
Multiple independent tasks shall not be combined within one specification.
---
### Clarity
Task requirements shall be complete, measurable, and unambiguous.
Every task shall be understandable before execution begins.
---
### Alignment
Task specifications shall remain aligned with approved production plans, schedules, resource planning, and production
standards.
---
### Traceability
Every task shall remain traceable to approved production planning, supporting assets, and resulting production records.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Production Task Specification establishes the following operational components.
### Task Identification
Each production task shall receive a unique Task Identifier.
The identifier supports governance, reporting, scheduling, and operational traceability.
---
### Task Definition
Defines the operational purpose and expected outcome of the task.
---
### Inputs
Defines the information, assets, resources, or approvals required before the task begins.
---
### Outputs
Defines the production deliverables produced by successful completion of the task.

---
### Task Requirements
Defines the operational requirements governing task execution.
Requirements establish expected outcomes rather than execution procedures.
---
### Dependencies
Defines production activities or deliverables that must exist before the task may begin.
---
### Acceptance Criteria
Defines measurable conditions used to verify successful completion of the task.
Acceptance criteria support verification rather than approval.
---
### Execution Constraints
Defines approved limitations affecting task execution, including organizational, technical, creative, operational, or
production constraints.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
Every significant production activity shall:
• Possess one approved Production Task Specification.
• Define one operational responsibility.
• Establish measurable completion criteria.
• Remain aligned with approved production documentation.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Production Task Specification is responsible for:
• Defining production tasks.
• Defining task requirements.
• Defining task inputs and outputs.
• Defining task dependencies.
• Defining acceptance criteria.
• Supporting production execution.

It is not responsible for:
• Scheduling work.
• Assigning personnel.
• Producing assets.
• Managing production execution.
• Approving completed work.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## 6. Operational Constraints
The Production Task Specification shall not:
• Replace the Production Plan.
• Replace the Production Schedule.
• Replace the Asset Specification.
• Replace the Production Checklist.
• Replace production execution management.
Its responsibility is limited to defining one approved production task.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Production Task Specification establishes the official operational definition of an individual production task within an
Explorer Studio production.
It defines the task's purpose, requirements, inputs, outputs, dependencies, acceptance criteria, and execution constraints
while ensuring that every production activity possesses one clearly defined operational responsibility.
The approved Production Task Specification becomes the authoritative specification for production task definition while
preserving the Single Source of Truth for all production task requirements throughout the production lifecycle.

# EXPLORER STUDIO PRODUCTION DOCUMENTATION SYSTEM
# PART IV — PRODUCTION DOCUMENTS
# Document 37 — Asset Specification
---
## Purpose
The Asset Specification defines the official engineering specification for an individual production asset within an Explorer Studio
production.
Its purpose is to define the functional purpose, characteristics, requirements, constraints, acceptance criteria, and relationships
of a production asset before the asset enters production.
The Asset Specification serves as the authoritative definition of one production asset.
It defines production assets.
It does not create assets, register assets, manage asset production, or control asset versions.
This document establishes the official production asset specification standard for every Explorer Studio production.
---
## Scope
This document applies to every official Explorer Studio production.
It governs production asset specification only.
It does not replace the Asset Register, Production Task Specification, Production Checklist, production repositories, or asset
lifecycle management.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## When Used
This document shall be used:
• Before production asset creation.
• Before asset review.
• Before asset registration.
• During production execution.
• Whenever a new production asset is required.
• Whenever an approved asset specification requires revision.

---

## Usage Rules
Every significant production asset shall possess one approved Asset Specification.
Each Asset Specification shall define one production asset.

---
## Governance Notes
This document owns the operational standard for production asset specification.
No other production document may redefine or replace this responsibility.
The Asset Specification defines production assets.
It does not register, produce, or manage production assets.
---
## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:
• Specify assets before production begins.
• Define measurable asset requirements.
• Maintain clear acceptance criteria.
• Reference supporting production documentation.

• Preserve complete asset traceability.
• Keep one specification for one asset.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Defines one production asset.
• Establishes asset requirements.
• Defines asset characteristics.
• Defines acceptance criteria.
• Supports asset production.
• Establishes the official asset definition.
---
# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Asset Specification
The Asset Specification establishes the official engineering definition of an individual production asset.
It provides the operational information necessary to create, review, verify, and manage the asset consistently throughout the
production lifecycle.
The Asset Specification defines **what the production asset shall be**.
It does not define **how the asset is produced or managed after creation**.
---
## 2. Core Principles
The Asset Specification operates according to the following principles.
### Single Responsibility
Each Asset Specification shall define one production asset.
Multiple independent assets shall not share the same specification.
---
### Clarity
Asset requirements shall be complete, measurable, and unambiguous.
Every asset shall be sufficiently specified before production begins.
---
### Alignment

Asset specifications shall remain aligned with approved Production Task Specifications, production plans, and production
standards.
---
### Traceability
Every asset specification shall remain traceable to its originating production task, supporting documentation, resulting
production asset, and Asset Register entry.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Asset Specification establishes the following operational components.
### Asset Identification
Each production asset shall receive a unique Asset Identifier.
The identifier supports governance, production coordination, registration, and long-term maintenance.
---
### Asset Definition
Defines the intended purpose and operational function of the asset.
---
### Asset Category
Defines the approved classification of the production asset.
Classification supports organization and management throughout production.
---
### Asset Requirements
Defines the operational, creative, technical, and production requirements governing the asset.
Requirements define expected characteristics rather than production methods.
---
### Asset Characteristics
Defines measurable attributes of the production asset, including where applicable:
• Functional characteristics
• Creative characteristics
• Technical characteristics
• Operational characteristics
---
### Dependencies

Defines production tasks, assets, approvals, or documentation required before the asset may be produced.
---
### Acceptance Criteria
Defines measurable conditions used to verify that the completed asset satisfies its approved specification.
Acceptance criteria support verification rather than approval.
---
### Asset Constraints
Defines approved limitations affecting the production asset, including technical, creative, legal, organizational, or operational
constraints.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
Every significant production asset shall:
• Possess one approved Asset Specification.
• Possess one unique Asset Identifier.
• Define measurable requirements.
• Define measurable acceptance criteria.
• Remain aligned with approved production documentation.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Asset Specification is responsible for:
• Defining production assets.
• Defining asset requirements.
• Defining asset characteristics.
• Defining asset dependencies.
• Defining acceptance criteria.
• Supporting production asset creation.
It is not responsible for:
• Producing assets.
• Registering assets.
• Managing asset versions.
• Managing repositories.
• Managing production execution.

Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## 6. Operational Constraints
The Asset Specification shall not:
• Replace the Asset Register.
• Replace Production Task Specifications.
• Replace Production Checklists.
• Replace version control systems.
• Replace production asset repositories.
Its responsibility is limited to defining one approved production asset.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Asset Specification establishes the official engineering definition of an individual production asset within an Explorer Studio
production.
It defines the asset's purpose, requirements, characteristics, dependencies, acceptance criteria, and operational constraints
while ensuring that every production asset possesses one complete and authoritative specification before production begins.
The approved Asset Specification becomes the authoritative specification for production asset definition while preserving the
Single Source of Truth for all production asset requirements throughout the production lifecycle.

## Purpose
The Asset Register defines the official inventory of production assets for an Explorer Studio production.
Its purpose is to identify, classify, record, monitor, and maintain every approved production asset throughout its lifecycle,
ensuring complete visibility, ownership, traceability, version awareness, and operational control.
The Asset Register serves as the authoritative inventory of production assets.
It records production assets.
It does not define asset requirements, create assets, approve assets, or manage asset production workflows.
This document establishes the official production asset inventory for every Explorer Studio production.

## Scope

It governs production asset registration and inventory management only.
It does not replace Asset Specifications, production execution, version control systems, configuration management, or
production archives.

It does not define, create, approve, or modify production assets.
---
## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:
• Register assets immediately after approval.
• Use unique asset identifiers.

• Maintain current version references.
• Preserve ownership information.
• Keep storage references accurate.
• Preserve complete asset traceability throughout the production lifecycle.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Records all approved production assets.
• Maintains unique asset identification.
• Maintains ownership and status.
• Records current version references.
• Supports production governance.
• Establishes the official production asset inventory.
---
# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Asset Register
The Asset Register establishes the authoritative inventory of production assets used throughout an Explorer Studio
production.
It provides one controlled location for recording every approved production asset and its operational information.
The Asset Register defines **what production assets officially exist**.
It does not define **how production assets are created or produced**.
---
## 2. Core Principles
The Asset Register operates according to the following principles.
### Single Source of Truth
Every approved production asset shall possess one official Asset Register entry.
Duplicate inventory records shall not exist.
---
### Traceability
Every registered asset shall remain traceable throughout its complete lifecycle.
Traceability shall include ownership, status, version, storage location, and related documentation.

---
### Accountability
Every production asset shall identify one operational owner responsible for maintaining its registration information.
---
### Consistency
Assets shall be classified, identified, and maintained using approved production standards.
Equivalent assets shall receive equivalent documentation.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Asset Register establishes the following operational components.
### Asset Identification
Each production asset shall receive a unique Asset Identifier.
The identifier supports governance, production coordination, and long-term maintenance.
---
### Asset Classification
Each asset shall belong to one approved production asset category.
Classification supports organization, reporting, and retrieval.
---
### Asset Ownership
Every asset shall identify one operational owner.
Ownership supports accountability for asset maintenance.
---
### Asset Status
Each asset shall maintain an approved operational status, including where applicable:
• Planned
• In Development
• Under Review
• Approved
• Released
• Archived
Additional approved organizational statuses may be adopted where appropriate.

---
### Version Reference
Each asset shall reference its current approved version.
Detailed version management remains governed by version control procedures.
---
### Storage Reference
Each asset shall identify its approved storage or repository location.
Storage references support discoverability and operational access.
---
### Related Documentation
Each asset shall reference supporting production documentation where applicable.
Cross-referencing supports documentation integrity.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
Every approved production asset shall:
• Possess one Asset Register entry.
• Possess one unique Asset Identifier.
• Maintain current ownership.
• Maintain current status.
• Maintain current version information.
Asset information shall remain synchronized with approved production documentation.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Asset Register is responsible for:
• Recording production assets.
• Recording asset ownership.
• Recording asset status.
• Recording version references.
• Recording storage locations.
• Supporting production asset governance.

It is not responsible for:
• Defining asset requirements.
• Creating production assets.
• Reviewing production assets.
• Approving production assets.
• Managing production workflows.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## 6. Operational Constraints
The Asset Register shall not:
• Replace the Asset Specification.
• Replace version control systems.
• Replace production repositories.
• Replace production archives.
• Replace production execution documentation.
Its responsibility is limited to maintaining the official inventory of production assets.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Asset Register establishes the official production asset inventory for an Explorer Studio production.
It records every approved production asset, maintains ownership, classification, operational status, version references,
storage information, and documentation relationships while preserving complete traceability throughout the production
lifecycle.
The approved Asset Register becomes the authoritative specification for production asset inventory management while
preserving the Single Source of Truth for all production assets used within the production.

# EXPLORER STUDIO PRODUCTION DOCUMENTATION SYSTEM

# Document 39 — Production Checklist
---
## Purpose
The Production Checklist defines the official operational verification checklist used to confirm that production activities have
been completed according to approved production requirements.
Its purpose is to provide a standardized verification mechanism that supports consistency, completeness, accountability, and
quality throughout production execution.
The Production Checklist verifies completion.
It does not define production requirements, replace task specifications, approve deliverables, or perform quality assurance.
This document establishes the official production verification checklist for every Explorer Studio production.
---
## Scope
This document applies to every official Explorer Studio production.
It governs production activity verification only.
It does not replace Production Task Specifications, Quality Checklists, Validation Reports, Production Reviews, or Approval
Records.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## When Used
This document shall be used:
• During production execution.
• Before milestone completion.
• Before internal reviews.
• Before approval activities.
• Before production handoffs.
• Whenever standardized operational verification is required.

• Verification Items
• Verification Status

• Verification Date
• Reviewer
• Completion Status
• Verification Notes
---
## Optional Sections

• Attachments

• Follow-up Actions
• Department References
Optional sections shall supplement the approved checklist without replacing required verification information.
---
## Standard Document Structure
The Production Checklist shall follow the standardized metadata architecture established by the Production Documentation
System.
The operational specification defined by this document supplements that architecture.
---
## Lifecycle
The Production Checklist progresses through:
• Creation
• Operational Use

• Verification
• Review
• Completion
• Archiving
Completed checklists become permanent production records supporting operational traceability.
---
## Revision Rules
This document may be revised only to:
• Reflect approved production procedure changes.
• Improve verification clarity.
• Correct approved information.
• Support approved governance improvements.
Completed checklists shall not be modified except through approved administrative correction procedures.
---
## Usage Rules
Every standardized production activity requiring formal verification shall use an approved Production Checklist.
Verification shall occur only after the applicable production activity has been completed.
Checklist completion shall accurately reflect the verified operational state.
---
## Governance Notes
This document owns the operational standard for production activity verification.
No other production document may redefine or replace this responsibility.
The Production Checklist verifies completion of production activities.
It does not approve production deliverables or replace quality verification.
---
## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:
• Use standardized checklists.
• Verify every required item individually.
• Record verification immediately after completion.
• Preserve objective verification evidence.

• Maintain complete operational traceability.
• Archive completed checklists as production records.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Verifies all required production items.
• Records responsible personnel.
• Records verification dates.
• Documents completion status.
• Supports production governance.
• Establishes verified operational completion.
---
# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Production Checklist
The Production Checklist establishes the official operational verification mechanism for production activities.
It provides standardized verification that required production work has been completed according to approved production
standards before subsequent activities proceed.
The Production Checklist defines **what has been operationally verified**.
It does not define **whether the work satisfies quality or approval requirements**.
---
## 2. Core Principles
The Production Checklist operates according to the following principles.
### Completeness
Every required verification item shall be evaluated before the checklist is considered complete.
Incomplete verification shall remain clearly identified.
---
### Consistency
Equivalent production activities shall use equivalent verification methods.
Standardized checklists support operational consistency across productions.
---
### Accountability

Every completed checklist shall identify the responsible verifier.
Verification responsibility supports production accountability.
---
### Traceability
Completed checklists shall remain traceable to the production activity, task specification, responsible personnel, and
verification date.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Production Checklist establishes the following operational components.
### Checklist Identification
Each checklist shall receive a unique identifier supporting governance and historical traceability.
---
### Applicable Activity
Identifies the production activity being verified.
The checklist shall verify one clearly defined operational activity.
---
### Verification Items
Defines the individual operational requirements requiring verification.
Each item shall be independently verified.
---
### Verification Status
Each verification item shall record its operational result, including where applicable:
• Completed
• Not Completed
• Not Applicable
• Requires Follow-up
Additional approved organizational statuses may be adopted where appropriate.
---
### Responsible Person
Identifies the individual responsible for performing operational verification.
---

### Completion Status
Records the overall operational completion status of the checklist.
Completion indicates verification—not approval.
---
### Verification Notes
Records operational observations supporting verification activities.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
Production Checklists shall be completed:
• Before production handoffs.
• Before production reviews.
• Before milestone completion.
• Before approval activities where required.
Verification shall be evidence-based and objectively performed.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Production Checklist is responsible for:
• Verifying production activities.
• Recording verification results.
• Recording responsible personnel.
• Recording completion status.
• Supporting operational traceability.
It is not responsible for:
• Defining production tasks.
• Performing quality assurance.
• Approving deliverables.
• Managing production execution.
• Authorizing production progression.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---

## 6. Operational Constraints
The Production Checklist shall not:
• Replace Production Task Specifications.
• Replace Quality Checklists.
• Replace Validation Reports.
• Replace Approval Records.
• Replace Production Reviews.
Its responsibility is limited to verifying completion of defined production activities.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Production Checklist establishes the official operational verification standard for an Explorer Studio production.
It provides a structured method for confirming completion of production activities by recording verification items,
responsible personnel, completion status, supporting notes, and operational evidence.
The approved Production Checklist becomes the authoritative verification record supporting production consistency,
governance, operational traceability, and successful progression through the production lifecycle while preserving the Single
Source of Truth for production activity verification.

# EXPLORER STUDIO PRODUCTION DOCUMENTATION SYSTEM
# PART IV — PRODUCTION DOCUMENTS
# Document 40 — Daily Production Log
---
## Purpose
The Daily Production Log defines the official daily operational record of production activities for an Explorer Studio
production.
Its purpose is to record factual information about production work performed during each production day, providing a
chronological operational history that supports coordination, reporting, governance, auditing, and long-term institutional
knowledge.
The Daily Production Log records daily production activities.
It does not manage production execution, approve work, evaluate performance, or replace production reporting.
This document establishes the official daily production record for every Explorer Studio production.
---
## Scope
This document applies to every official Explorer Studio production.
It governs daily production logging only.
It does not replace Production Progress Reports, Weekly Production Reports, Meeting Records, Decision Logs, Issue Registers,
or Change Logs.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## When Used
This document shall be used:
• Throughout active production execution.
• At the end of each production day.
• Before preparing weekly production reports.
• During production monitoring.
• During production reviews.
• During production audits.
---

Explorer Studio
Production Manager
---
## Contributors

• Documentation Manager

• Assigned Production Personnel
• Future authorized production roles

## Approval Authority

## Required Sections

• Activities Completed
• Activities In Progress
• Assets Created or Updated
• Issues Observed
• Decisions Referenced
• Schedule Notes
• Team Participation
• Operational Notes
---
## Optional Sections
Where appropriate, the Daily Production Log may additionally include:
• Weather or Environmental Notes
• Equipment Availability
• External Dependencies
• Supporting References
• Additional Observations
Optional sections shall supplement the approved log without replacing required operational information.
---
## Standard Document Structure
The Daily Production Log shall follow the standardized metadata architecture established by the Production Documentation
System.
The operational specification defined by this document supplements that architecture.
---
## Lifecycle
The Daily Production Log progresses through:

• Daily Creation
• Operational Review
• Completion
• Historical Preservation
• Archiving
Each completed Daily Production Log becomes a permanent chronological production record.
---
## Revision Rules
This document may be revised only to:
• Correct approved factual errors.
• Add omitted approved operational information.
• Correct administrative information.
Historical production activities shall never be rewritten to alter the production record.
Every revision shall remain documented and traceable.
---
## Usage Rules
Every active production day shall generate one official Daily Production Log.
Logs shall record factual operational information only.
Forecasts, assumptions, and personal opinions shall not be recorded as production facts.
---
## Governance Notes
This document owns the operational standard for daily production recording.
No other production document may redefine or replace this responsibility.
The Daily Production Log records daily production history.
It does not evaluate production performance or authorize production decisions.
---
## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:
• Complete the log before the production day concludes.
• Record activities objectively.
• Record information consistently.

• Preserve chronological order.
• Cross-reference related production records where appropriate.
• Maintain complete historical traceability.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Records all significant daily production activities.
• Records operational observations.
• References significant issues and decisions.
• Preserves factual production history.
• Supports governance and reporting.
• Establishes the official daily operational record.
---
# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Daily Production Log
The Daily Production Log establishes the official chronological record of daily production activities.
It documents factual operational information generated during each production day and preserves an accurate historical
record supporting production governance and institutional knowledge.
The Daily Production Log defines **what occurred during a production day**.
It does not define **how production performance should be evaluated**.
---
## 2. Core Principles
The Daily Production Log operates according to the following principles.
### Accuracy
Entries shall accurately reflect verified production activities.
Only factual operational information shall be recorded.
---
### Timeliness
Daily Production Logs shall be completed as close as practical to the conclusion of each production day.
Timely recording improves information accuracy.
---

### Chronology
Entries shall preserve the chronological sequence of production activities.
Historical order shall remain intact throughout the production lifecycle.
---
### Traceability
Every significant activity recorded within the log shall remain traceable to related production documentation where
applicable.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Daily Production Log establishes the following operational components.
### Production Day Identification
Each log shall identify one specific production day.
One production day shall have one official Daily Production Log.
---
### Activity Recording
Records completed production activities performed during the day.
Activities shall remain factual and concise.
---
### Work in Progress
Records significant production activities continuing beyond the current production day.
---
### Asset Activity
Records production assets created, modified, reviewed, or completed during the reporting period.
Authoritative asset information remains owned by the Asset Register.
---
### Operational Observations
Records notable operational events affecting production.
Observations provide historical context.
---
### Cross References
Records references to related:

• Decisions
• Issues
• Meetings
• Approved Changes
Cross-references support documentation integrity without duplicating information.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
One Daily Production Log shall be created for every active production day.
Production information shall be recorded objectively and chronologically.
Information recorded within the Daily Production Log shall support later production reporting without replacing it.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Daily Production Log is responsible for:
• Recording daily production activities.
• Recording work in progress.
• Recording operational observations.
• Recording production history.
• Supporting production reporting.
• Supporting long-term traceability.
It is not responsible for:
• Evaluating production performance.
• Approving production work.
• Managing production execution.
• Managing production issues.
• Authorizing production decisions.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## 6. Operational Constraints
The Daily Production Log shall not:
• Replace the Weekly Production Report.

• Replace the Production Status Report.
• Replace the Decision Log.
• Replace the Issue Register.
• Replace production governance.
Its responsibility is limited to maintaining the official daily operational history of production activities.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Daily Production Log establishes the official daily operational record for an Explorer Studio production.
It records factual production activities, work in progress, asset activity, operational observations, and references to related
production records while preserving a complete chronological history of production execution.
The approved Daily Production Log becomes the authoritative specification for daily production recording while preserving
the Single Source of Truth for day-to-day production history throughout the production lifecycle.

# EXPLORER STUDIO PRODUCTION DOCUMENTATION SYSTEM

# Document 41 — Weekly Production Report
---
## Purpose
The Weekly Production Report defines the official weekly summary of production performance for an Explorer Studio
production.
Its purpose is to consolidate the operational activities, progress, achievements, issues, risks, approved changes, milestone
status, and planned work completed during a defined reporting week into one standardized management report.
The Weekly Production Report provides operational visibility across the production.
It summarizes production performance.
It does not replace Daily Production Logs, Production Status Reports, Production Reviews, or executive governance.
This document establishes the official weekly production reporting standard for every Explorer Studio production.

## Scope

It governs weekly production reporting only.
It does not replace daily operational records, issue management, change management, production planning, or executive
decision-making.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.

## When Used

• At the conclusion of each production week.
• Before weekly production meetings.
• Before governance reviews.
• Before executive reporting.

## Lifecycle

## Revision Rules

• Add omitted verified information.

---
## Usage Rules
Every active production week shall generate one official Weekly Production Report.
Reports shall summarize verified operational information only.
Weekly reports shall consolidate—not duplicate—daily production records.
---
## Governance Notes
This document owns the operational standard for weekly production reporting.
No other production document may redefine or replace this responsibility.
The Weekly Production Report summarizes weekly production activity.
It does not authorize production decisions or replace executive reporting.
---
## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:

• Prepare reports at consistent weekly intervals.
• Base summaries on verified operational records.
• Highlight significant production events.
• Reference supporting production documentation.
• Preserve concise management reporting.
• Maintain complete historical traceability.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Summarizes weekly production activities.
• Reports milestone progress.
• Summarizes significant issues and risks.
• Summarizes approved changes.
• Supports production governance.

---
# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Weekly Production Report
The Weekly Production Report establishes the official weekly operational summary of production.
It consolidates verified production information into one standardized management report supporting governance,
coordination, executive oversight, and historical documentation.
The Weekly Production Report defines **what occurred during a production week**.
It does not define **overall production health or executive decisions**.
---
## 2. Core Principles
The Weekly Production Report operates according to the following principles.
### Consolidation
The report shall consolidate verified information from approved production records.
It shall avoid unnecessary duplication.
---
### Accuracy

Weekly summaries shall accurately represent verified production information.
Only factual operational information shall be reported.
---
### Relevance
Reports shall emphasize operationally significant production events requiring management awareness.
Routine operational detail shall remain within Daily Production Logs.
---
### Traceability
Every significant statement within the report shall remain traceable to supporting production documentation.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Weekly Production Report establishes the following operational components.
### Reporting Week
Defines the production week covered by the report.
---
### Executive Summary
Provides a concise summary of the week's production performance.
---
### Production Progress
Summarizes measurable production advancement achieved during the reporting period.
---
### Major Activities
Summarizes significant production work completed and currently underway.
---
### Milestone Progress
Summarizes progress against scheduled production milestones.
Detailed milestone ownership remains with the Production Schedule.
---
### Significant Decisions
Summarizes major production decisions made during the reporting period.

Authoritative decision records remain within the Decision Log.
---
### Significant Issues
Summarizes operational issues requiring management awareness.
Detailed issue ownership remains with the Issue Register.
---
### Significant Risks
Summarizes risks materially affecting production.
Authoritative risk information remains within the Risk Register.
---
### Approved Changes
Summarizes implemented approved production changes affecting the reporting period.
Detailed change history remains within the Change Log.
---
### Planned Activities
Summarizes significant work planned for the upcoming reporting period.
This section communicates approved planning rather than creating new plans.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
One Weekly Production Report shall be prepared for every active production week.
Weekly reporting shall summarize verified production information using approved organizational reporting standards.
Reports shall preserve historical accuracy.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Weekly Production Report is responsible for:
• Summarizing weekly production progress.
• Reporting milestone progress.
• Reporting significant production events.
• Reporting summary-level issues, risks, and changes.
• Supporting production governance.
• Preserving weekly production history.

It is not responsible for:
• Recording daily operational activities.
• Managing production execution.
• Approving production decisions.
• Managing production risks.
• Managing production issues.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## 6. Operational Constraints
The Weekly Production Report shall not:
• Replace the Daily Production Log.
• Replace the Production Status Report.
• Replace the Production Review Report.
• Replace the Issue Register.
• Replace executive governance.
Its responsibility is limited to providing the official weekly summary of production activities.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Weekly Production Report establishes the official weekly production reporting standard for an Explorer Studio
production.
It consolidates verified operational information from daily production records into a structured weekly management report
summarizing production progress, milestone status, significant decisions, issues, risks, approved changes, and planned work.
The approved Weekly Production Report becomes the authoritative weekly production summary while preserving the Single
Source of Truth for weekly production reporting throughout the production lifecycle.

## Required Sections
The Decision Log shall include, where applicable:
• Decision Identifier
• Decision Title
• Decision Description
• Decision Date
• Decision Authority
• Decision Category
• Decision Rationale
• Related Documents
• Implementation Reference
• Decision Status
---
## Optional Sections
Where appropriate, the Decision Log may additionally include:
• Alternatives Considered
• Related Risks
• Related Issues
• Supporting Evidence
• Historical Notes
Optional sections shall supplement the approved decision record without replacing required decision information.
---
## Standard Document Structure
The Decision Log shall follow the standardized metadata architecture established by the Production Documentation System.
The operational specification defined by this document supplements that architecture.
---
## Lifecycle
The Decision Log progresses through:
• Decision Recording

• Operational Reference
• Historical Maintenance
• Archiving
Each recorded decision becomes a permanent part of the official production history.
---
## Revision Rules
This document may be revised only to:
• Correct approved factual information.
• Record implementation references.
• Update administrative information.
Historical decisions shall never be altered to change the original approved decision.
Every revision shall remain documented and traceable.
---
## Usage Rules
Every significant production decision shall be recorded within the Decision Log.
Only approved decisions shall be entered.
Informal discussions and unapproved proposals shall not be recorded as production decisions.
---
## Governance Notes
This document owns the operational standard for production decision recording.
No other production document may redefine or replace this responsibility.
The Decision Log records approved production decisions.
It does not approve, implement, or evaluate those decisions.
---
## Best Practices
Explorer Studio productions should:
• Record decisions immediately after approval.
• Preserve decision rationale.
• Reference supporting documentation.
• Maintain chronological order.
• Preserve historical integrity.

• Maintain complete organizational traceability.
---
## Completion Criteria
This document is considered complete when it:
• Records every significant approved production decision.
• Preserves decision rationale.
• Maintains implementation references.
• Supports governance and auditing.
• Preserves production history.
• Establishes the official production decision record.
---
# Operational Specification
## 1. Purpose of the Decision Log
The Decision Log establishes the official historical record of approved production decisions.
It preserves organizational knowledge by documenting what decisions were made, why they were made, when they were
approved, and which production activities they affected.
The Decision Log defines **what production decisions have been officially made**.
It does not define **how those decisions are implemented**.
---
## 2. Core Principles
The Decision Log operates according to the following principles.
### Historical Integrity
Every approved production decision shall remain permanently recorded.
Decision history shall never be rewritten.
---
### Accountability
Every decision shall identify the authority responsible for approving it.
Decision ownership supports governance accountability.
---
### Traceability
Every decision shall remain traceable to:

• Governance activities.
• Supporting documentation.
• Related production documents.
• Implementation references where applicable.
---
### Consistency
Production decisions shall be recorded using one standardized organizational method.
Equivalent decisions shall receive equivalent documentation.
---
## 3. Operational Model
The Decision Log establishes the following operational components.
### Decision Identification
Each decision shall receive a unique Decision Identifier.
The identifier supports governance, reporting, and historical reference.
---
### Decision Classification
Each decision shall belong to one approved decision category.
Classification supports reporting and organizational analysis.
---
### Decision Authority
Every recorded decision shall identify the authority responsible for approving the decision.
Authority supports governance traceability.
---
### Decision Rationale
Records the operational reasoning supporting the approved decision.
The rationale provides historical context.
---
### Related Documentation
Records references to production documents affected by or supporting the decision.
Cross-references strengthen documentation integrity.

---
### Implementation Reference
Where applicable, records the production documents or operational activities implementing the approved decision.
Implementation remains governed elsewhere.
---
### Decision Status
Each recorded decision shall maintain an approved status, including where applicable:
• Approved
• Superseded
• Withdrawn
• Completed
Additional approved organizational statuses may be adopted where appropriate.
---
## 4. Operational Rules
Every significant production decision shall:
• Be approved through appropriate governance.
• Be recorded promptly after approval.
• Remain permanently preserved.
Decision history shall remain complete throughout the production lifecycle.
---
## 5. Operational Responsibilities
The Decision Log is responsible for:
• Recording approved production decisions.
• Preserving decision history.
• Recording decision rationale.
• Recording decision authority.
• Maintaining documentation references.
• Supporting governance and historical traceability.
It is not responsible for:
• Conducting meetings.
• Approving decisions.

• Implementing decisions.
• Managing production execution.
• Managing organizational governance.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## 6. Operational Constraints
The Decision Log shall not:
• Replace Meeting Records.
• Replace Change Requests.
• Replace Change Logs.
• Replace Production Governance.
• Replace Production Reviews.
Its responsibility is limited to maintaining the official historical record of approved production decisions.
---
## 7. Operational Summary
The Decision Log establishes the official historical record of production decisions within an Explorer Studio production.
It records every significant approved decision, preserves decision rationale, identifies approving authority, maintains
implementation references, and supports governance, auditing, organizational learning, and long-term production
knowledge.
The approved Decision Log becomes the authoritative specification for production decision history while preserving the
Single Source of Truth for all approved production decisions throughout the production lifecycle.

# EXPLORER STUDIO PRODUCTION DOCUMENTATION SYSTEM
# PART IV — PRODUCTION DOCUMENTS
# Document 43 — Issue Register
---
## Purpose
The Issue Register defines the official operational record of production issues for an Explorer Studio production.
Its purpose is to record, classify, prioritize, assign ownership, monitor, and track all operational issues that arise during the
production lifecycle until their resolution or formal closure.
The Issue Register serves as the authoritative repository for active production issues.
It supports operational visibility, production coordination, governance, and decision-making.
It records production issues.
It does not perform risk assessment, approve corrective actions, authorize changes, or manage production execution.
This document establishes the official production issue management baseline for every Explorer Studio production.
---
## Scope
This document applies to every official Explorer Studio production.
It governs production issue registration and tracking only.
It does not replace the Risk Register, Change Management, Production Progress Reporting, Production Governance, or
Production Execution.
Those responsibilities belong to their respective production documents.
---
## When Used
This document shall be used:
• After production execution begins.
• Whenever operational issues are identified.
• During production monitoring.
• During governance reviews.
• During production reporting.
• Until all recorded issues are formally resolved or closed.

## Lifecycle

## Revision Rules

• Close resolved issues.
