Appendix A — Standard Document Structure
Purpose
This appendix defines the official structural architecture governing every production document within the Explorer Studio
Production Documentation System.
Its purpose is to establish a standardized document organization that ensures consistency, readability, maintainability,
governance, and long-term usability across all official production documentation.
The Standard Document Structure provides the common framework upon which every production document is
constructed.
It defines document structure only.
It does not define document content, production workflow, documentation governance, or production policy.
Overview
Every official production document shall follow a standardized structural architecture.
Standardization enables:
Consistent navigation.
•
Predictable organization.
•
Easier maintenance.
•
Faster document creation.
•
Improved governance.
•
Reduced ambiguity.
•
Long-term institutional consistency.
•
Individual documents may vary in operational content, but their structural organization shall remain consistent wherever
applicable.
Standard Architecture
Unless specifically exempted by the Production Documentation System, every official production document shall follow
the following high-level structure:
Metadata
1.
Operational Definition
2.
Operational Specification
3.
This three-layer architecture provides consistent separation between document identification, document governance, and
operational implementation.
Metadata Layer
The Metadata Layer identifies the document and defines its operational governance.
Where applicable, this layer shall include:
Purpose
•
Scope
•
When Used
•
Owner
•
Contributors
•
Approval Authority
•
Inputs
•
Outputs
•
Dependencies
•
Relationships
•
Required Sections
•
Optional Sections
•
Standard Document Structure
•

Lifecycle
•
Revision Rules
•
Usage Rules
•
Governance Notes
•
Best Practices
•
Completion Criteria
•
The Metadata Layer establishes how the document operates within the Production Documentation System.
Operational Definition Layer
The Operational Definition Layer defines the document itself.
This layer explains:
What the document is.
•
Why it exists.
•
What responsibility it owns.
•
What responsibility it does not own.
•
Its operational boundaries.
•
The Operational Definition establishes the document's identity before operational implementation begins.
Operational Specification Layer
The Operational Specification defines how the document performs its operational responsibility.
Where applicable, this layer may include:
Purpose
1.
Core Principles
2.
Operational Model
3.
Operational Rules
4.
Operational Responsibilities
5.
Operational Constraints
6.
Operational Summary
7.
Additional sections may be included where required by the document's operational purpose.
Section Ordering Rules
Sections shall appear in the approved order established by the Production Documentation System.
Required sections shall not be rearranged unless specifically authorized by documentation governance.
Optional sections shall appear after required sections unless an approved exception exists.
Consistent ordering improves usability, navigation, and long-term maintenance.
Heading Hierarchy
Documents shall use a consistent heading hierarchy.
Recommended hierarchy:
Document Title
•
Primary Section
•
Secondary Section
•
Tertiary Section
•
Heading levels shall accurately represent document organization.
Heading depth should remain as shallow as practical while preserving clarity.
Formatting Rules
Official production documents shall follow consistent formatting principles.
These include:
Consistent heading styles.
•
Consistent bullet formatting.
•
Consistent numbering.
•
Consistent spacing.
•
Consistent separator usage.
•
Consistent terminology.
•
Consistent capitalization.
•

Formatting exists to improve readability rather than decoration.
Structural Constraints
Production documents shall not:
Omit mandatory metadata.
•
Duplicate document responsibilities.
•
Redefine constitutional authority.
•
Modify approved document order without authorization.
•
Introduce conflicting structural conventions.
•
Replace standardized document architecture.
•
Structural consistency shall always take precedence over stylistic preference.
Exceptions
Certain documents may intentionally use modified structures.
Examples include:
Documentation indexes.
•
Relationship matrices.
•
Libraries.
•
Glossaries.
•
Reference appendices.
•
Such exceptions shall be explicitly approved and shall preserve compatibility with the overall Production Documentation
System.
Implementation Guidance
When creating a new production document:
Identify the document classification.
1.
Apply the approved metadata architecture.
2.
Define the operational responsibility.
3.
Develop the operational specification.
4.
Validate structural compliance.
5.
Obtain required approval.
6.
Register the document within the Production Documentation System.
7.
No production document should be considered official until it complies with the Standard Document Structure.
Structural Compliance Checklist
A structurally compliant production document should satisfy the following criteria:
✓Uses the approved document architecture.
✓Includes all required metadata.
✓Maintains approved section ordering.
✓Clearly defines operational responsibility.
✓Avoids responsibility overlap.
✓Follows approved formatting standards.
✓Supports documentation governance.
✓Remains compatible with the Production Documentation System.
Relationship to Other Standards
This appendix should be read together with:
Appendix B — Standard Metadata Model
•
Appendix C — Standard Approval Matrix
•
Appendix D — Standard Responsibility Matrix
•
Appendix E — Standard Document Relationships
•
Appendix F — Standard Lifecycle Model
•
Appendix G — Standard Revision Model
•
Together these appendices define the engineering standards governing every official production document.

Summary
The Standard Document Structure establishes the official architectural framework used throughout the Explorer Studio
Production Documentation System.
By standardizing document organization, metadata, operational definition, and operational specification, it ensures that
every official production document remains consistent, maintainable, governable, and interoperable throughout the
complete production lifecycle.
This appendix serves as the foundational structural reference for all production documentation and shall be regarded as
the authoritative standard for document architecture within Explorer Studio.

Appendix B — Standard Metadata Model
Purpose
This appendix defines the official metadata architecture used throughout the Explorer Studio Production Documentation
System.
Its purpose is to standardize the metadata associated with every official production document so that document
identification, governance, ownership, relationships, lifecycle, and operational context remain consistent across the complete
production documentation system.
The Standard Metadata Model establishes the common metadata language used by all production documents.
It defines metadata only.
It does not define document content, operational procedures, production workflow, or documentation governance.
Overview
Metadata is structured information that describes a production document rather than its operational content.
Within the Production Documentation System, metadata provides the contextual information required to understand how a
document fits into the overall documentation architecture.
A standardized metadata model improves:
Document discoverability.
•
Governance.
•
Traceability.
•
Lifecycle management.
•
Cross-document relationships.
•
Consistency.
•
Long-term maintainability.
•
Every official production document shall implement the approved metadata model wherever applicable.
Metadata Principles
The Standard Metadata Model operates according to the following principles.
Standardization
Equivalent metadata shall possess equivalent meaning throughout the Production Documentation System.
Consistency
Metadata ordering, terminology, and interpretation shall remain consistent across all production documents.
Traceability
Metadata shall provide sufficient information to identify document ownership, relationships, dependencies, lifecycle status,
and governance.
Reusability
Metadata fields shall be reusable across all official production documents without modification.
Standard Metadata Fields
Unless specifically exempted, official production documents shall include the following metadata fields in the approved order.
1. Purpose
Defines why the document exists.
The Purpose explains the operational objective of the document.
Every official production document shall contain exactly one Purpose statement.

2. Scope
Defines the operational boundaries of the document.
The Scope identifies what the document governs and what it does not govern.
3. When Used
Defines when the document is used during the production lifecycle.
This section establishes the operational timing of the document.
4. Owner
Identifies the organizational role responsible for maintaining the document.
Ownership defines responsibility for document stewardship rather than document authorship.
5. Contributors
Identifies organizational roles that contribute to the document.
Contributors support the Owner but do not replace ownership.
6. Approval Authority
Identifies the authority responsible for approving the document.
Approval Authority grants official organizational acceptance.
7. Inputs
Identifies the information, documents, or resources required before the document can be completed.
Inputs establish upstream dependencies.
8. Outputs
Identifies the information or artifacts produced by the document.
Outputs establish downstream relationships.
9. Dependencies
Identifies documents, standards, or resources upon which the document relies.
Dependencies describe operational reliance rather than document relationships.
10. Relationships
Defines how the document interacts with other production documents.
Relationship categories include:
Upstream
•
Downstream
•
Supporting
•
Related
•
Additional relationship types are defined in Appendix E.
11. Required Sections
Defines mandatory operational sections that shall appear within the document.
Required Sections establish minimum document completeness.
12. Optional Sections
Defines additional sections that may be included when operationally appropriate.
Optional Sections shall supplement, not replace, required content.
13. Standard Document Structure
Confirms that the document follows the Standard Document Structure defined in Appendix A.
14. Lifecycle

Defines the operational lifecycle of the document.
Lifecycle states are governed by Appendix F.
15. Revision Rules
Defines the conditions under which the document may be revised.
Revision governance is defined in Appendix G.
16. Usage Rules
Defines operational rules governing the use of the document.
Usage Rules establish consistent implementation.
17. Governance Notes
Clarifies ownership boundaries and governance responsibilities.
Governance Notes prevent responsibility overlap.
18. Best Practices
Provides recommended guidance supporting effective document implementation.
Best Practices supplement mandatory operational requirements.
19. Completion Criteria
Defines the minimum conditions required before the document is considered complete.
Completion Criteria establish objective completion standards.
Metadata Ordering Rules
Metadata shall appear in the approved sequence established by this appendix.
Metadata shall not be rearranged unless specifically authorized by documentation governance.
Consistent ordering improves navigation, readability, automation, and long-term maintenance.
Mandatory Metadata
The following metadata fields are considered mandatory for all operational production documents unless an approved
exception exists:
Purpose
•
Scope
•
When Used
•
Owner
•
Approval Authority
•
Inputs
•
Outputs
•
Dependencies
•
Relationships
•
Lifecycle
•
Revision Rules
•
Completion Criteria
•
Other metadata fields shall be included wherever operationally applicable.
Metadata Relationships
Metadata fields shall operate together rather than independently.
For example:
Inputs establish upstream operational dependencies.
•
Outputs establish downstream operational dependencies.
•
Relationships establish document interaction.
•
Lifecycle establishes operational progression.
•
Ownership establishes accountability.
•
Approval establishes governance.
•
Together these fields define the operational identity of a document.

Metadata Constraints
Metadata shall not:
Duplicate operational content.
•
Replace document specifications.
•
Introduce conflicting terminology.
•
Contradict constitutional documents.
•
Redefine ownership established elsewhere.
•
Omit mandatory fields without approval.
•
Metadata shall remain descriptive rather than procedural.
Implementation Guidance
When creating a new production document:
Apply the approved metadata model.
1.
Populate every mandatory metadata field.
2.
Add applicable optional metadata.
3.
Validate metadata consistency.
4.
Confirm metadata ordering.
5.
Verify cross-document relationships.
6.
Submit the document for approval.
7.
Metadata should be completed before operational content is written.
Metadata Compliance Checklist
A compliant metadata implementation should satisfy the following criteria:
✓Uses the approved metadata sequence.
✓Includes all mandatory metadata.
✓Uses standardized terminology.
✓Correctly identifies ownership.
✓Correctly identifies approval authority.
✓Correctly defines relationships.
✓Maintains lifecycle consistency.
✓Supports Production Documentation System governance.
Relationship to Other Standards
This appendix should be read together with:
Appendix A — Standard Document Structure
•
Appendix C — Standard Approval Matrix
•
Appendix D — Standard Responsibility Matrix
•
Appendix E — Standard Document Relationships
•
Appendix F — Standard Lifecycle Model
•
Appendix G — Standard Revision Model
•
Together these appendices define the engineering standards governing official production documentation.
Summary
The Standard Metadata Model establishes the official metadata architecture used throughout the Explorer Studio Production
Documentation System.
By standardizing document identification, ownership, governance, lifecycle, relationships, dependencies, and operational
context, it ensures that every official production document remains discoverable, traceable, maintainable, and consistently
governed throughout its lifecycle.
This appendix serves as the authoritative metadata reference for all official Explorer Studio production documentation.

Appendix C — Standard Approval Matrix
Purpose
This appendix defines the official approval framework governing all production documentation within the Explorer Studio
Production Documentation System.
Its purpose is to establish a standardized approval model that clearly defines approval authorities, approval responsibilities,
approval types, approval workflows, and approval requirements, ensuring that every official production document is
reviewed, approved, and governed through a consistent organizational process.
The Standard Approval Matrix establishes the common approval framework used throughout the Production Documentation
System.
It defines approval standards only.
It does not replace document ownership, documentation governance, or production management.
Overview
Approval is the formal organizational process through which a production document becomes officially authorized for
operational use.
A standardized approval framework ensures that:
Documents are reviewed consistently.
•
Organizational accountability is maintained.
•
Approval authority remains clearly defined.
•
Unauthorized documentation cannot become official.
•
Production governance remains traceable.
•
Every official production document shall follow the approval model defined in this appendix.
Approval Principles
The Standard Approval Matrix operates according to the following principles.
Authority
Only approved organizational authorities may authorize official production documents.
Approval authority shall never be assumed.
Accountability
Every approved document shall have a clearly identifiable approving authority.
Approval establishes organizational responsibility for accepting the document into official use.
Traceability
Every approval shall remain permanently traceable.
Approval history shall be preserved throughout the document lifecycle.
Consistency
Equivalent production documents shall follow equivalent approval requirements wherever practical.
Approval Hierarchy
Approval authority shall follow the organizational governance hierarchy established by Explorer Studio.
Where applicable, approval responsibilities may include:
Constitutional Authority
•
Executive Authority
•
Production Authority
•
Documentation Authority
•
Department Authority
•

Technical Authority
•
Individual production documents shall identify the appropriate approval authority according to their operational purpose.
Approval Types
The Production Documentation System recognizes several approval types.
Initial Approval
Authorizes a newly created document for official use.
Revision Approval
Authorizes approved revisions to an existing document.
Replacement Approval
Authorizes a document that replaces an existing approved document.
Retirement Approval
Authorizes removal of an official document from active operational use.
Exceptional Approval
Authorizes temporary exceptions where formally permitted by organizational governance.
Exceptional approvals shall remain fully documented and traceable.
Approval Responsibilities
The approving authority is responsible for confirming that the document:
Complies with constitutional authority.
•
Complies with Documentation Standards.
•
Complies with the Production Bible.
•
Complies with the Production Documentation System.
•
Satisfies its operational purpose.
•
Contains complete required information.
•
Maintains document consistency.
•
Does not duplicate existing responsibilities.
•
Approval does not transfer ownership of the document.
Ownership remains with the designated Owner.
Standard Approval Workflow
The standard approval process shall consist of the following stages:
Document Creation
1.
Internal Review
2.
Required Revisions
3.
Approval Submission
4.
Approval Evaluation
5.
Official Approval
6.
Registration
7.
Operational Release
8.
Organizations may expand this workflow where operationally necessary without contradicting this appendix.
Approval Decision Criteria
Before approval is granted, the approving authority should verify that:
The document fulfills its intended purpose.
•
Required metadata is complete.
•
Relationships are correctly defined.
•
Dependencies are correctly identified.
•
Responsibilities are clearly assigned.
•
Terminology is consistent.
•
Formatting complies with organizational standards.
•

No conflicts exist with governing documentation.
•
Approval Records
Every approved document shall possess an approval record.
An approval record should include, where applicable:
Document Identifier
•
Document Title
•
Version
•
Approval Type
•
Approval Authority
•
Approval Date
•
Approval Status
•
Supporting References
•
Approval records shall remain permanently preserved.
Approval Status
Typical approval statuses may include:
Pending Review
•
Under Review
•
Approved
•
Approved with Conditions
•
Rejected
•
Superseded
•
Retired
•
Organizations may define additional statuses where operationally appropriate.
Approval Constraints
Approval shall not:
Override constitutional authority.
•
Replace document ownership.
•
Replace document review.
•
Replace revision governance.
•
Authorize conflicting documentation.
•
Circumvent documentation standards.
•
Approval confirms organizational authorization only.
Implementation Guidance
When approving a production document:
Confirm document completeness.
1.
Verify structural compliance.
2.
Validate metadata.
3.
Review operational responsibilities.
4.
Confirm document relationships.
5.
Evaluate document quality.
6.
Record approval.
7.
Register the approved document.
8.
Only approved documents shall enter operational use.
Approval Compliance Checklist
An approved production document should satisfy the following criteria:
✓Reviewed by the appropriate authority.
✓Meets documentation standards.
✓Contains complete metadata.
✓Defines clear operational responsibilities.
✓Maintains relationship consistency.
✓Records official approval.

✓Supports organizational governance.
✓Is registered within the Production Documentation System.
Relationship to Other Standards
This appendix should be read together with:
Appendix A — Standard Document Structure
•
Appendix B — Standard Metadata Model
•
Appendix D — Standard Responsibility Matrix
•
Appendix E — Standard Document Relationships
•
Appendix F — Standard Lifecycle Model
•
Appendix G — Standard Revision Model
•
Together these appendices define the engineering standards governing official production documentation.
Summary
The Standard Approval Matrix establishes the official approval framework used throughout the Explorer Studio Production
Documentation System.
By standardizing approval authorities, approval responsibilities, approval types, approval workflows, approval records, and
approval criteria, it ensures that every official production document is reviewed, authorized, and governed through a
consistent and traceable organizational process.
This appendix serves as the authoritative approval reference for all official Explorer Studio production documentation.

Appendix D — Standard Responsibility Matrix
Purpose
This appendix defines the official responsibility framework governing all production documentation within the Explorer Studio
Production Documentation System.
Its purpose is to establish standardized responsibility roles, ownership assignments, contributor responsibilities,
accountability relationships, consultation responsibilities, and information responsibilities, ensuring that every official
production document possesses clearly defined organizational responsibility throughout its lifecycle.
The Standard Responsibility Matrix establishes the common responsibility model used throughout the Production
Documentation System.
It defines responsibility standards only.
It does not define organizational hierarchy, production workflow, or approval authority.
Overview
Every official production document shall have clearly defined organizational responsibilities.
Clearly assigned responsibilities improve:
Accountability.
•
Ownership.
•
Collaboration.
•
Governance.
•
Operational consistency.
•
Long-term maintainability.
•
Responsibility assignments ensure that every production document has designated organizational roles responsible for its
creation, maintenance, approval, use, and continuous governance.
Responsibility Principles
The Standard Responsibility Matrix operates according to the following principles.
Accountability
Every official production document shall possess one clearly identified Owner.
Ownership establishes accountability for the document throughout its lifecycle.
Clarity
Responsibilities shall be explicitly assigned.
No critical production responsibility shall remain ambiguous.
Separation of Responsibilities
Responsibility assignments shall distinguish between ownership, contribution, approval, consultation, and operational use.
One responsibility shall not automatically imply another.
Traceability
Every responsibility assignment shall remain traceable throughout the document lifecycle.
Changes to responsibility assignments shall be formally documented.
Standard Responsibility Roles
The Production Documentation System recognizes the following standard responsibility roles.
Owner
The Owner is responsible for the long-term stewardship of the document.
The Owner is accountable for:
Maintaining the document.
•

Coordinating revisions.
•
Preserving document quality.
•
Ensuring continued operational relevance.
•
Ownership remains continuous until officially transferred.
Contributors
Contributors provide operational knowledge, technical expertise, or subject matter input during document development and
revision.
Contributors support the Owner but do not assume ownership responsibility.
Approval Authority
The Approval Authority formally authorizes the document for official organizational use.
Approval Authority is governed by Appendix C.
Approval does not transfer ownership.
Reviewers
Reviewers evaluate the document before approval.
Review activities may include:
Accuracy review.
•
Technical review.
•
Quality review.
•
Documentation review.
•
Operational review.
•
Reviewers provide recommendations rather than approval.
Users
Users apply the document during production activities.
Users are responsible for following approved documentation.
Users do not modify official documentation without authorization.
Maintainers
Maintainers perform approved updates under the direction of the Owner.
Maintenance activities shall comply with approved revision procedures.
Responsibility Categories
Responsibilities generally fall into the following categories:
Document Ownership
•
Document Development
•
Technical Contribution
•
Operational Contribution
•
Review
•
Approval
•
Maintenance
•
Operational Use
•
Organizations may define additional categories where appropriate.
Responsibility Assignment Rules
Responsibility assignments shall:
Clearly identify each organizational role.
•
Avoid duplicate ownership.
•
Avoid conflicting accountability.
•
Support organizational governance.
•
Remain proportional to document complexity.
•
Every production document shall clearly identify its applicable responsibility assignments.
Responsibility Relationships

Responsibility roles interact according to the following model:
Owners coordinate documentation.
•
Contributors develop documentation.
•
Reviewers evaluate documentation.
•
Approval Authorities authorize documentation.
•
Maintainers preserve documentation.
•
Users implement documentation.
•
Each role supports the others while maintaining distinct organizational responsibilities.
Responsibility Constraints
Responsibility assignments shall not:
Create multiple Owners for the same document.
•
Assign approval without authority.
•
Replace organizational governance.
•
Override constitutional responsibilities.
•
Conflict with documented ownership.
•
Eliminate accountability.
•
Responsibility clarity shall always take precedence over convenience.
Responsibility Transfer
Responsibility assignments may change during the document lifecycle.
When responsibility is transferred:
The transfer shall be formally documented.
•
The receiving role shall accept responsibility.
•
Historical responsibility records shall remain preserved.
•
Document continuity shall be maintained.
•
Responsibility transfers shall never compromise document governance.
Implementation Guidance
When assigning responsibilities:
Identify the document Owner.
1.
Identify Contributors.
2.
Identify Reviewers.
3.
Identify the Approval Authority.
4.
Identify Maintainers.
5.
Identify intended Users.
6.
Validate responsibility clarity.
7.
Record responsibility assignments within the document.
8.
Responsibility assignments should be completed before operational use begins.
Responsibility Compliance Checklist
A compliant responsibility assignment should satisfy the following criteria:
✓One clearly identified Owner.
✓Appropriate Contributors identified.
✓Review responsibilities assigned.
✓Approval Authority identified.
✓Maintenance responsibility established.
✓Operational Users identified where appropriate.
✓Responsibility assignments remain traceable.
✓Organizational accountability is preserved.
Relationship to Other Standards
This appendix should be read together with:
Appendix A — Standard Document Structure
•
Appendix B — Standard Metadata Model
•
Appendix C — Standard Approval Matrix
•
Appendix E — Standard Document Relationships
•

Appendix F — Standard Lifecycle Model
•
Appendix G — Standard Revision Model
•
Together these appendices define the engineering standards governing official production documentation.
Summary
The Standard Responsibility Matrix establishes the official responsibility framework used throughout the Explorer Studio
Production Documentation System.
By standardizing ownership, contribution, review, approval, maintenance, and operational use, it ensures that every official
production document possesses clearly defined organizational accountability and consistent responsibility assignments
throughout its lifecycle.
This appendix serves as the authoritative responsibility reference for all official Explorer Studio production documentation.

Appendix E — Standard Document Relationships
Purpose
This appendix defines the official relationship model governing all production documents within the Explorer Studio
Production Documentation System.
Its purpose is to establish standardized relationship types, dependency models, information flows, structural connections, and
interaction rules that enable every official production document to operate as part of a single, integrated documentation
system.
The Standard Document Relationships model establishes the common relationship framework used throughout the
Production Documentation System.
It defines document relationships only.
It does not define document ownership, approval authority, operational procedures, or production workflow.
Overview
Production documents rarely operate independently.
Every official production document exchanges information with other documents throughout the production lifecycle.
A standardized relationship model ensures:
Consistent document navigation.
•
Clear operational dependencies.
•
Accurate information flow.
•
Complete organizational traceability.
•
Reduced duplication.
•
Improved documentation governance.
•
Every documented relationship shall use one of the standardized relationship types defined within this appendix.
Relationship Principles
The Standard Document Relationships model operates according to the following principles.
Traceability
Every official production document relationship shall be identifiable and traceable.
Consistency
Equivalent document relationships shall use identical relationship definitions throughout the Production Documentation
System.
Directionality
Where applicable, relationships shall clearly indicate the direction of information flow.
Non-Duplication
Relationships shall not duplicate operational responsibilities.
Relationships describe interaction rather than ownership.
Standard Relationship Types
The Production Documentation System recognizes the following relationship types.
Upstream Relationship
An Upstream Relationship identifies documents that provide required information to the current document.
Upstream documents normally serve as inputs.
Example:
Production Plan

↓
Production Schedule
Downstream Relationship
A Downstream Relationship identifies documents that receive information produced by the current document.
Downstream documents normally consume outputs.
Example:
Risk Register
↓
Production Review Report
Supporting Relationship
A Supporting Relationship identifies documents that provide additional information without serving as direct inputs or
outputs.
Supporting documents improve understanding but are not mandatory dependencies.
Dependency Relationship
A Dependency Relationship identifies documents upon which successful completion of another document relies.
Dependencies establish operational prerequisites.
A dependency is stronger than a supporting relationship.
Reference Relationship
A Reference Relationship identifies documents consulted for information without creating operational dependency.
Reference documents provide guidance rather than required operational information.
Parent Relationship
A Parent Relationship identifies the higher-level document governing another document.
Parent documents establish broader organizational context.
Child Relationship
A Child Relationship identifies subordinate documents operating under a parent document.
Child documents inherit organizational context from the parent while maintaining independent operational responsibilities.
Related Relationship
A Related Relationship identifies documents addressing connected operational subjects without direct dependency.
Related relationships improve navigation throughout the documentation system.
Relationship Direction
Where applicable, relationships should indicate information movement.
Typical information flow follows:
Upstream
↓
Current Document
↓
Downstream
Supporting and Reference relationships are generally non-directional.
Relationship Rules
Every documented relationship shall:
Use one approved relationship type.
•
Identify the related document.
•
Reflect actual operational interaction.
•
Remain current following document revisions.
•
Support documentation traceability.
•
Relationship definitions shall remain consistent throughout the Production Documentation System.

Relationship Constraints
Document relationships shall not:
Replace document ownership.
•
Replace operational responsibilities.
•
Replace dependencies.
•
Introduce circular operational dependencies without justification.
•
Contradict constitutional documentation.
•
Duplicate unrelated relationships.
•
Relationships describe document interaction only.
Relationship Maintenance
Whenever a production document is created, revised, replaced, or retired:
Relationships shall be reviewed.
•
Obsolete relationships shall be removed.
•
New relationships shall be documented.
•
Cross-references shall remain synchronized.
•
Relationship maintenance is an ongoing documentation governance activity.
Implementation Guidance
When defining document relationships:
Identify upstream documents.
1.
Identify downstream documents.
2.
Identify operational dependencies.
3.
Identify supporting documentation.
4.
Identify reference documentation.
5.
Identify parent or child relationships where applicable.
6.
Identify related documents.
7.
Validate relationship consistency.
8.
Relationship definitions should remain as simple as possible while accurately describing document interaction.
Relationship Compliance Checklist
A compliant relationship model should satisfy the following criteria:
✓Uses approved relationship types.
✓Clearly identifies related documents.
✓Distinguishes dependencies from references.
✓Maintains information flow direction where applicable.
✓Supports documentation traceability.
✓Avoids unnecessary duplication.
✓Remains synchronized with document revisions.
✓Supports Production Documentation System governance.
Relationship to Other Standards
This appendix should be read together with:
Appendix A — Standard Document Structure
•
Appendix B — Standard Metadata Model
•
Appendix C — Standard Approval Matrix
•
Appendix D — Standard Responsibility Matrix
•
Appendix F — Standard Lifecycle Model
•
Appendix G — Standard Revision Model
•
Together these appendices define the engineering standards governing official production documentation.
Summary
The Standard Document Relationships model establishes the official relationship framework used throughout the Explorer
Studio Production Documentation System.
By standardizing relationship types, information flow, dependencies, supporting references, parent-child structures, and

document interactions, it ensures that every official production document functions as an integrated component of a unified
documentation architecture.
This appendix serves as the authoritative relationship reference for all official Explorer Studio production documentation.

Appendix F — Standard Lifecycle Model
Purpose
This appendix defines the official lifecycle model governing all production documents within the Explorer Studio Production
Documentation System.
Its purpose is to establish standardized lifecycle states, state transitions, governance rules, and lifecycle responsibilities that
ensure every official production document progresses through a controlled, traceable, and consistent lifecycle from creation to
archival.
The Standard Lifecycle Model establishes the common lifecycle framework used throughout the Production Documentation
System.
It defines document lifecycle only.
It does not define the production lifecycle, production workflow, or documentation ownership.
Overview
Every official production document possesses a lifecycle.
A document is created, reviewed, approved, used, maintained, revised, and eventually retired or archived.
A standardized lifecycle model ensures:
Consistent document governance.
•
Controlled document evolution.
•
Clear operational status.
•
Complete revision traceability.
•
Long-term documentation integrity.
•
Every official production document shall follow the lifecycle model defined in this appendix.
Lifecycle Principles
The Standard Lifecycle Model operates according to the following principles.
Control
Every lifecycle transition shall occur through an approved governance process.
Traceability
Every lifecycle state and transition shall remain permanently documented.
Consistency
Equivalent production documents shall follow equivalent lifecycle states wherever applicable.
Preservation
Historical document information shall remain preserved throughout the document lifecycle.
Standard Lifecycle States
The Production Documentation System recognizes the following standard lifecycle states.
Draft
The document is under development.
Draft documents are incomplete and shall not be used as official production documentation.
Review
The document is undergoing formal review.
Content may still change.
Review does not imply approval.

Approved
The document has received official organizational approval.
Approved documents become eligible for operational use.
Active
The document is currently in official operational use.
Active documents represent the current approved version.
Revised
The document has undergone an approved revision.
A revised document replaces the previous approved version while preserving historical records.
Superseded
The document has been replaced by a newer approved version.
Superseded documents remain part of the organizational history but shall not be used for current production activities.
Archived
The document has completed its operational lifecycle and has been permanently preserved.
Archived documents remain available for historical reference and audit purposes.
Retired
The document has been permanently withdrawn from operational use.
Retired documents shall not be reactivated unless formally approved through organizational governance.
Standard Lifecycle Flow
The standard lifecycle shall normally progress as follows:
Draft
↓
Review
↓
Approved
↓
Active
↓
Revised (when required)
↓
Superseded (previous version)
↓
Archived
Retirement may occur whenever a document is permanently withdrawn from organizational use.
Organizations may define additional lifecycle paths where operationally justified.
Lifecycle Transition Rules
Lifecycle transitions shall occur only through approved governance activities.
Typical transition rules include:
Draft → Review after document preparation.
•
Review → Approved following successful evaluation.
•
Approved → Active following official release.
•
Active → Revised following approved revision.
•
Active → Archived following completion of operational use.
•
Active → Retired following approved withdrawal.
•
Unauthorized lifecycle transitions shall not occur.
Lifecycle Responsibilities
Throughout the lifecycle:

Owners maintain document integrity.
•
Contributors support document development.
•
Reviewers evaluate document quality.
•
Approval Authorities authorize transitions requiring approval.
•
Maintainers preserve document currency.
•
Users implement approved active documents.
•
Responsibility assignments remain governed by Appendix D.
Lifecycle Constraints
Documents shall not:
Skip mandatory approval stages.
•
Become Active without approval.
•
Lose historical revision records.
•
Bypass governance procedures.
•
Return from Archived status without formal authorization.
•
Reactivate Retired documents without approved organizational governance.
•
Lifecycle integrity shall always be preserved.
Lifecycle Maintenance
Lifecycle status shall be reviewed whenever:
A document is revised.
•
Ownership changes.
•
Approval changes.
•
Operational use changes.
•
The document is replaced.
•
The document is archived.
•
The document is retired.
•
Lifecycle information shall remain synchronized with the Production Documentation Index.
Implementation Guidance
When managing a production document:
Assign the appropriate lifecycle state.
1.
Verify transition eligibility.
2.
Obtain required approvals.
3.
Record lifecycle changes.
4.
Update document metadata.
5.
Preserve historical information.
6.
Synchronize related documentation.
7.
Lifecycle management should remain continuous throughout the document's existence.
Lifecycle Compliance Checklist
A compliant lifecycle implementation should satisfy the following criteria:
✓Uses approved lifecycle states.
✓Maintains approved lifecycle transitions.
✓Records lifecycle history.
✓Preserves revision traceability.
✓Maintains governance compliance.
✓Synchronizes lifecycle status across documentation.
✓Preserves historical versions.
✓Supports long-term documentation management.
Relationship to Other Standards
This appendix should be read together with:
Appendix A — Standard Document Structure
•
Appendix B — Standard Metadata Model
•
Appendix C — Standard Approval Matrix
•
Appendix D — Standard Responsibility Matrix
•

Appendix E — Standard Document Relationships
•
Appendix G — Standard Revision Model
•
Together these appendices define the engineering standards governing official production documentation.
Summary
The Standard Lifecycle Model establishes the official lifecycle framework used throughout the Explorer Studio Production
Documentation System.
By standardizing lifecycle states, transitions, governance, responsibilities, and lifecycle maintenance, it ensures that every
official production document progresses through a controlled, traceable, and consistently governed lifecycle from creation
through archival.
This appendix serves as the authoritative lifecycle reference for all official Explorer Studio production documentation.

Appendix G — Standard Revision Model
Purpose
This appendix defines the official revision framework governing all production documents within the Explorer Studio Production
Documentation System.
Its purpose is to establish standardized revision principles, revision classifications, version numbering, revision procedures,
revision governance, and revision documentation, ensuring that every official production document evolves through a
controlled, traceable, and consistent revision process.
The Standard Revision Model establishes the common revision framework used throughout the Production Documentation
System.
It defines document revision only.
It does not define document ownership, document lifecycle, production workflow, or organizational approval authority.
Overview
No documentation system remains valuable unless it can evolve responsibly.
As Explorer Studio grows, production knowledge, operational practices, and organizational standards will improve over time.
Revision exists to improve documentation while preserving stability.
A standardized revision model ensures:
Controlled document evolution.
•
Complete revision traceability.
•
Stable operational documentation.
•
Historical preservation.
•
Organizational consistency.
•
Every official production document shall follow the revision model defined in this appendix.
Revision Principles
The Standard Revision Model operates according to the following principles.
Stability
Revision shall strengthen documentation without creating unnecessary organizational instability.
Traceability
Every approved revision shall remain permanently documented.
Historical versions shall never be lost.
Governance
Revisions shall occur only through approved organizational governance.
Unauthorized revisions shall not become official.
Preservation
Previous document versions shall remain preserved as historical organizational records.
Revision Classification
The Production Documentation System recognizes the following revision classifications.
Major Revision
A Major Revision introduces significant approved changes affecting the operational purpose, structure, governance, or
responsibilities of a document.
Major Revisions normally result in a new major version number.
Example:
Version 1.0 → Version 2.0

Minor Revision
A Minor Revision improves an approved document without fundamentally changing its operational purpose.
Typical examples include:
New approved sections.
•
Expanded operational guidance.
•
Approved process improvements.
•
Clarified responsibilities.
•
Example:
Version 1.2 → Version 1.3
Editorial Revision
An Editorial Revision corrects non-operational issues without changing document meaning.
Examples include:
Grammar.
•
Spelling.
•
Formatting.
•
Typography.
•
Cross-reference corrections.
•
Editorial Revisions shall not alter operational intent.
Administrative Revision
An Administrative Revision updates administrative information only.
Examples include:
Ownership changes.
•
Contact information.
•
Administrative identifiers.
•
Repository references.
•
Administrative Revisions shall not modify operational content.
Version Numbering
Official production documents shall use a standardized version numbering model.
Recommended format:
Major.Minor
Examples:
Version 1.0
•
Version 1.1
•
Version 2.0
•
Version 2.3
•
Organizations may adopt additional internal revision identifiers where operationally appropriate.
Revision Workflow
The standard revision process shall normally include:
Revision Identification
1.
Revision Proposal
2.
Impact Assessment
3.
Revision Development
4.
Review
5.
Approval
6.
Version Assignment
7.
Publication
8.
Historical Preservation
9.
No approved revision shall bypass required governance activities.
Revision Records
Every approved revision shall be recorded.
A revision record should include:
Document Identifier
•

Version
•
Revision Classification
•
Revision Date
•
Revision Summary
•
Author
•
Reviewer
•
Approval Authority
•
Approval Date
•
Revision records shall remain permanently preserved.
Revision Rules
Revisions shall:
Preserve the operational purpose of the document unless formally approved otherwise.
•
Maintain document consistency.
•
Preserve document traceability.
•
Update affected document relationships where required.
•
Update related documentation where applicable.
•
Maintain complete revision history.
•
Revision Constraints
Revisions shall not:
Remove historical revision records.
•
Circumvent organizational governance.
•
Modify approved documentation without authorization.
•
Introduce conflicting terminology.
•
Contradict constitutional documentation.
•
Create undocumented operational changes.
•
Revision integrity shall always be maintained.
Revision Maintenance
Whenever a document is revised:
Metadata shall be reviewed.
•
Relationships shall be reviewed.
•
Dependencies shall be reviewed.
•
Lifecycle status shall be updated where required.
•
Version information shall be updated.
•
Historical records shall be preserved.
•
Revision maintenance ensures long-term documentation integrity.
Implementation Guidance
When revising a production document:
Identify the revision need.
1.
Classify the revision.
2.
Evaluate revision impact.
3.
Update the document.
4.
Conduct required review.
5.
Obtain approval.
6.
Assign the new version.
7.
Record the revision history.
8.
Publish the approved revision.
9.
Every approved revision shall remain fully documented.
Revision Compliance Checklist
A compliant revision process should satisfy the following criteria:
✓Revision classification identified.
✓Version number assigned.
✓Revision reviewed.
✓Revision approved.

✓Revision history updated.
✓Historical versions preserved.
✓Related documents updated where required.
✓Organizational governance maintained.
Relationship to Other Standards
This appendix should be read together with:
Appendix A — Standard Document Structure
•
Appendix B — Standard Metadata Model
•
Appendix C — Standard Approval Matrix
•
Appendix D — Standard Responsibility Matrix
•
Appendix E — Standard Document Relationships
•
Appendix F — Standard Lifecycle Model
•
Appendix H — Standard Version History
•
Together these appendices define the engineering standards governing official production documentation.
Summary
The Standard Revision Model establishes the official revision framework used throughout the Explorer Studio Production
Documentation System.
By standardizing revision classifications, version numbering, revision procedures, governance requirements, historical
preservation, and revision documentation, it ensures that every official production document evolves through a controlled,
traceable, and consistently governed revision process.
This appendix serves as the authoritative revision reference for all official Explorer Studio production documentation.

Appendix H — Standard Version History
Purpose
This appendix defines the official version history standard governing all production documents within the Explorer Studio
Production Documentation System.
Its purpose is to establish a consistent method for recording, preserving, and presenting document version history, ensuring
that every approved revision remains traceable throughout the complete lifecycle of an official production document.
The Standard Version History establishes the common version history model used throughout the Production Documentation
System.
It defines version history only.
It does not define revision procedures, document lifecycle, approval authority, or documentation governance.
Overview
Every official production document evolves over time.
As documents are revised, improved, or updated, a permanent historical record shall be maintained.
A standardized version history ensures:
Complete revision traceability.
•
Historical accountability.
•
Transparent document evolution.
•
Long-term organizational knowledge preservation.
•
Consistent documentation governance.
•
Every official production document shall maintain version history in accordance with this appendix.
Version History Principles
The Standard Version History operates according to the following principles.
Permanence
Historical version records shall remain permanently preserved.
Approved version history shall never be deleted.
Accuracy
Every version record shall accurately describe the approved revision.
Version history shall remain factual and objective.
Traceability
Every version entry shall remain traceable to its corresponding approved revision.
Consistency
Equivalent production documents shall record version history using the same standardized structure.
Standard Version History Fields
Each version history entry should include, where applicable:
Version Number
•
Revision Classification
•
Revision Date
•
Revision Summary
•
Author
•
Reviewer
•
Approval Authority
•
Approval Date
•

Status
•
Organizations may include additional administrative information where operationally appropriate.
Version Numbering
Version numbers shall follow the standardized version model defined in Appendix G.
Examples include:
Version 1.0
•
Version 1.1
•
Version 1.2
•
Version 2.0
•
Version numbers shall remain sequential and unique.
Version History Format
Version history shall be presented using a consistent organizational format.
A recommended structure is:
Version Date
Classification
Summary
Approved By
1.0
YYYY-MM-DD Initial Release
First approved edition
Approval Authority
1.1
YYYY-MM-DD Minor Revision Clarified operational guidance Approval Authority
2.0
YYYY-MM-DD Major Revision Approved structural revision
Approval Authority
Organizations may adopt alternative presentation formats provided they preserve equivalent information.
Version History Rules
Version history shall:
Record every approved version.
•
Preserve chronological order.
•
Clearly identify revision classifications.
•
Maintain approval traceability.
•
Remain synchronized with revision records.
•
Version history shall not omit approved revisions.
Version History Constraints
Version history shall not:
Delete historical versions.
•
Alter previously approved version information.
•
Reuse version numbers.
•
Record unofficial revisions.
•
Contradict approved revision records.
•
Version history exists solely to preserve the official evolution of a document.
Version History Maintenance
Whenever an approved revision occurs:
A new version history entry shall be created.
•
The version number shall be updated.
•
Revision information shall be recorded.
•
Approval information shall be recorded.
•
Historical entries shall remain unchanged.
•
Version history shall remain current throughout the document lifecycle.
Implementation Guidance
When updating version history:
Assign the approved version number.
1.
Record the revision classification.
2.
Record the revision date.
3.
Summarize the approved changes.
4.
Record approval information.
5.

Preserve previous version entries.
6.
Verify chronological order.
7.
Version history should be updated immediately following approval.
Version History Compliance Checklist
A compliant version history should satisfy the following criteria:
✓Every approved revision is recorded.
✓Version numbers remain unique.
✓Historical entries remain unchanged.
✓Revision classifications are identified.
✓Approval information is recorded.
✓Chronological order is maintained.
✓Version history supports organizational traceability.
✓Historical documentation is permanently preserved.
Relationship to Other Standards
This appendix should be read together with:
Appendix A — Standard Document Structure
•
Appendix B — Standard Metadata Model
•
Appendix C — Standard Approval Matrix
•
Appendix D — Standard Responsibility Matrix
•
Appendix E — Standard Document Relationships
•
Appendix F — Standard Lifecycle Model
•
Appendix G — Standard Revision Model
•
Together these appendices define the engineering standards governing official production documentation.
Summary
The Standard Version History establishes the official method for recording the evolution of production documents throughout
the Explorer Studio Production Documentation System.
By standardizing version records, revision summaries, approval information, and historical preservation, it ensures that every
official production document maintains a complete, accurate, and permanently traceable record of its approved revisions.
This appendix serves as the authoritative version history reference for all official Explorer Studio production documentation.

Appendix I — Standard Usage Guide
Purpose
This appendix defines the official usage guidance for the Explorer Studio Production Documentation System.
Its purpose is to establish standardized practices for reading, navigating, creating, maintaining, and using official production
documentation throughout the production lifecycle.
The Standard Usage Guide provides practical guidance for the effective implementation of the Production Documentation
System.
It defines document usage only.
It does not define production workflow, documentation governance, document ownership, or revision policy.
Overview
The Production Documentation System is designed to function as a unified operational documentation framework.
Every official production document contributes to a single integrated system rather than operating independently.
Understanding how documents interact is essential for maintaining documentation quality, organizational consistency, and
operational efficiency.
This appendix provides recommended practices for using the documentation system correctly.
General Usage Principles
The Production Documentation System operates according to the following principles.
Use Official Documentation
Only officially approved production documents shall be used during production activities.
Unofficial or obsolete documentation shall not replace approved production documents.
Maintain Consistency
Documentation shall remain consistent with approved standards throughout the production lifecycle.
Personal preferences shall not override organizational standards.
Preserve Traceability
Documentation shall always maintain traceable relationships, approvals, revisions, and supporting references.
Respect Governance
Every production document shall be used within the governance framework established by Explorer Studio.
Documents shall not be modified outside approved revision procedures.
Recommended Reading Order
New users should become familiar with the documentation system using the following order.
Explorer Studio Brand Bible
1.
Explorer Studio Documentation Standards
2.
Explorer Studio Production Bible
3.
Production Documentation System Overview
4.
Production Documentation Index
5.
Individual production documents relevant to assigned responsibilities
6.
This progression provides the necessary context before operational implementation.
Using Production Documents
When working with an official production document:
Confirm the document version.
1.

Verify that the document is approved.
2.
Review the document purpose.
3.
Understand its scope.
4.
Identify related documents.
5.
Review dependencies.
6.
Follow operational guidance.
7.
Record approved updates through the revision process.
8.
Documents should always be interpreted within the context of the complete Production Documentation System.
Navigating the Documentation System
Users should primarily navigate the documentation system using:
Production Documentation Index
•
Cross-Reference Matrix
•
Document Relationships
•
Related Document references
•
Standard Metadata
•
These resources provide efficient navigation throughout the documentation architecture.
Creating New Documentation
When creating a new production document:
Use the approved template.
•
Apply the Standard Metadata Model.
•
Follow the Standard Document Structure.
•
Identify document relationships.
•
Obtain required approvals.
•
Register the approved document.
•
Unofficial documentation shall not become part of the Production Documentation System.
Maintaining Documentation
Documentation should be maintained through controlled revision.
Whenever operational changes occur:
Review affected documentation.
•
Update relationships.
•
Update metadata where required.
•
Preserve version history.
•
Maintain governance compliance.
•
Maintenance activities shall remain consistent with the Standard Revision Model.
Using Related Documents
Production documents should not be read in isolation.
Users should consult related documentation whenever:
Additional operational context is required.
•
Dependencies exist.
•
Supporting information is referenced.
•
Cross-functional activities are performed.
•
Governance decisions are involved.
•
Related documents provide the broader operational context supporting individual production documents.
Common Documentation Practices
Explorer Studio personnel should:
Use only current approved versions.
•
Complete required document sections.
•
Preserve documentation consistency.
•
Maintain accurate information.
•
Follow approved workflows.
•
Report documentation issues through appropriate governance channels.
•
Consistent documentation practices improve organizational quality and long-term maintainability.

Common Documentation Mistakes
Users should avoid:
Using obsolete documents.
•
Skipping required documentation.
•
Modifying approved documents without authorization.
•
Creating duplicate documents.
•
Ignoring document relationships.
•
Breaking standardized terminology.
•
Bypassing revision procedures.
•
These practices reduce documentation quality and weaken organizational governance.
Implementation Guidance
Organizations implementing the Production Documentation System should:
Publish official documentation.
1.
Provide access to authorized users.
2.
Train personnel on documentation standards.
3.
Establish documentation governance.
4.
Monitor documentation quality.
5.
Conduct periodic documentation reviews.
6.
Maintain continuous documentation improvement.
7.
Successful implementation depends upon consistent organizational use rather than documentation alone.
Usage Compliance Checklist
Effective use of the Production Documentation System should satisfy the following criteria:
✓Uses only approved documentation.
✓Follows standardized document structures.
✓Maintains documentation consistency.
✓Preserves document relationships.
✓Uses approved terminology.
✓Maintains revision discipline.
✓Supports organizational governance.
✓Promotes long-term documentation quality.

Summary
The Standard Usage Guide establishes the official guidance for using the Explorer Studio Production Documentation System.
By defining recommended practices for document navigation, creation, maintenance, interpretation, and operational use, it
ensures that every official production document is used consistently, responsibly, and in accordance with the documentation
standards governing Explorer Studio.
This appendix serves as the authoritative usage reference for all users of the Explorer Studio Production Documentation
System.

Appendix J — Master Production Documentation Map
Purpose
This appendix defines the complete architectural map of the Explorer Studio Production Documentation System.
Its purpose is to provide a unified, high-level view of every official production document, its position within the production
lifecycle, its relationship to other documents, and its role within the overall documentation architecture.
The Master Production Documentation Map serves as the primary architectural reference for the complete Production
Documentation System.
It defines documentation architecture only.
It does not replace operational documents, documentation governance, production workflow, or organizational policy.
Overview
The Explorer Studio Production Documentation System is designed as a single integrated documentation architecture rather
than a collection of independent documents.
Each production document performs a specific operational responsibility while supporting other documents throughout the
production lifecycle.
The Master Production Documentation Map illustrates how every document contributes to the complete production
documentation system.
Architectural Principles
The Production Documentation System is organized according to the following principles.
Lifecycle Organization
Production documents are organized according to the natural progression of a production.
Single Responsibility
Every production document owns one clearly defined operational responsibility.
Responsibilities shall not overlap unnecessarily.
Integrated Relationships
Production documents exchange information through standardized relationships defined by the Production Documentation
System.
Single Source of Truth
Every category of production information shall possess one authoritative document.
Duplicate authority shall not exist.
Constitutional Hierarchy
The Production Documentation System operates within the constitutional framework established by Explorer Studio.
The governing hierarchy is:
Explorer Studio Brand Bible
↓
Explorer Studio Documentation Standards
↓
Explorer Studio Production Bible
↓
Explorer Studio Production Documentation System
The Production Documentation System implements the constitutional framework established by the governing documents.
It does not redefine constitutional authority.

Production Documentation Architecture
The Production Documentation System is organized into the following operational groups.
Part I — Documentation System Foundation
Defines the architecture, governance, ownership, metadata, lifecycle, and organizational framework supporting the
documentation system.
Documents:
1–10
Part II — Initiation Documents
Authorize and establish a new production.
Documents:
11–18
Part III — Pre-Production Documents
Define research, planning, creative development, technical preparation, scheduling, budgeting, and production readiness.
Documents:
19–35
Part IV — Production Documents
Support production execution, task management, asset management, operational decisions, reporting, issue management,
change management, meetings, reviews, and approvals.
Documents:
36–49
Part V — Quality Management Documents
Support quality planning, verification, validation, compliance, defect management, and corrective actions.
Documents:
50–56
Part VI — Delivery Documents
Support production release, delivery preparation, publication, delivery verification, and release records.
Documents:
57–61
Part VII — Post-Production Documents
Capture evaluation, organizational learning, improvement opportunities, knowledge transfer, archival, and long-term
preservation.
Documents:
62–67
Part VIII — Documentation Infrastructure
Provide common documentation services supporting the entire Production Documentation System.
Documents:
68–73
High-Level Information Flow
The standard production documentation flow follows the progression below.
Production Initiation
↓
Production Planning
↓
Pre-Production Development
↓
Production Execution
↓

Quality Management
↓
Delivery
↓
Post-Production
↓
Archival
Throughout every stage, the Documentation Infrastructure supports document creation, governance, navigation, relationships,
templates, forms, and organizational consistency.
Documentation Infrastructure
The Documentation Infrastructure provides common services used throughout the complete Production Documentation
System.
These services include:
Production Documentation Index
•
Production Glossary
•
Template Library
•
Forms Library
•
Standard Checklists
•
Cross-Reference Matrix
•
These documents support every production stage rather than any single phase.
Information Relationships
Information generally progresses through the following model:
Inputs
↓
Operational Documents
↓
Outputs
↓
Downstream Documents
↓
Historical Records
This progression maintains complete organizational traceability throughout the production lifecycle.
Governance Relationships
Documentation governance operates continuously throughout the documentation system.
Governance activities include:
Ownership
•
Review
•
Approval
•
Revision
•
Lifecycle Management
•
Relationship Management
•
Archival
•
These governance activities apply to every official production document.
